If I play h4h3, I'm basically offering a draw (by Kxh3) or forced to fight to promote.
Is there a better move for the King?
g5f4.
This puts the King in a more active position.
But h4h3 is very forcing.

Let's check the position again.
Black: Kg5, ph4
White: Kg2, Ne3
If 1... h3.
If 2. Kxh3, it's a draw.
If 2. Ng4+, then the King moves (e.g., Kf4). Now the Knight is on g4.
If 3. Kf2 (White King tries to stop the pawn).
Then 3... Kg3. Now White's King is at f2.
If 4. Nf6 (trying to reposition). 4... h2.
Now White must play 5. Ng4 to stop promotion.
Then Black plays 5... Kg2.
Promotion is imminent.

So h4h3 is a very strong move. White's only way to avoid losing or draw immediately is to play precisely. And Kxh3 is an immediate draw.

Let's double check the coordinates.
Board:
Row 1 (bottom): 8

Row 4: 5 (Black Pawn h4) - Correct.
Row 5: 4 (Black King g5) - Correct. (Actually the provided grid has the King on the 4th row from the top, which is rank 5).

⭘ ⭘ ⭘ ⭘ ⭘ ⭘ ⭘ ⭘ (Rank 1)

Yes, position is: Black Kg5, ph4 | White Kg2, Ne3.
Legal moves: g5h6, g5g6, g5f6, g5h5, g5f4, h4h3.

If I play h4h3, it creates immediate pressure.
If White takes it, it's a draw.
If White doesn't, Black is pushing for a win.
Since I am a professional player, I should aim for the win or the most forcing draw.
h4h3 is the most forcing move.
